The headline news of this cycle that the 6h-old cron missed: **`next@latest` flipped from `16.2.12` → `16.3.0` at npm-published 2026-08-03T21:03:18Z** — the **STABLE Next.js 16.3 release** tagged by Tim Neutkens himself (GitHub release tag `v16.3.0`, npm `dist-tag.latest` moved to `16.3.0`). The v16.3.0 release bundles 16 commits from the canary branch since canary.107, including the three material PRs that drove the canary.108 cycle: PR #96497 (`Enable TypeScript CLI by default` — `experimental.useTypeScriptCli` now default-`true` everywhere; TS 7 works out of the box), PR #96426 (`[Cache] Make caches error if called after prerender aborts` — fixes silent cache poisoning after prerender aborts under `cacheComponents: true`), PR #96308 (`Fix App Router scroll padding visibility` — sticky-header scroll-to-element fix), PR #93132 (`fix: double fragment on navigation` — closes #93126/#95551 hash concatenation regression), and 9+ additional bug fixes and perf wins. **The skill has been lagging on canary content for 7 days — `patterns.md` last updated 2026-07-28, `security.md` last updated 2026-07-29, `api.md` last updated 2026-07-29** — none of which knew about the upcoming v16.3.0 stable cut. **Then `next@canary` flipped from `16.3.0-canary.107` → `16.3.1-canary.0` at npm-published 2026-08-03T22:32:33Z** (just 88 minutes after v16.3.0 shipped) with a single commit: **PR #96553 by acdlite — `Fix catch-all index page being served for every other slug`** — a 16.3.0-introduced bug where catch-all routes (e.g., `/blog/[...slug]`, `/api/[...path]`) served the index handler (`slug = []` / `path = []`) for every URL matching the base path, instead of the proper dynamic page. The fix ships in 16.3.1-canary.0 immediately; expected to land in `16.3.1` stable shortly. **Three weakest areas identified — all in stale-stale files with the headline gap being the v16.3.0 STABLE release + canary-branch PR #96553**: **(1) `patterns.md` was last touched 2026-07-28 (7 days stale)** and was missing the **five flagship 16.3 patterns** every agent building a content-heavy Next.js app needs to know: **(a) TypeScript CLI is now the default — no flag, no `next.config.ts` line (PR #96497)** — the single biggest behavior change in 16.3.0 is invisible but TypeScript 7 works out of the box without `experimental.useTypeScriptCli: true`; **(b) ISR + Cache Components + Partial Prefetching (PR #96526)** — the canonical 16.3.0 architecture for content-heavy sites (`'use cache' + cacheLife('hours') + partialPrefetching: true` in `next.config.ts`); **(c) Cache-Poisoning After Prerender Abort — Fixed (PR #96426)** — silent cache corruption under `cacheComponents: true` + heavy `'use cache'` + frequent navigation aborts; **(d) `instant()` No Longer Implicitly Opts Into Partial Prefetching (PR #96539)** — subtle breaking change: `export const instant = true` is now shell-only and you must explicitly opt into `experimental.partialPrefetching: true`; **(e) Catch-All Index Page Served for Every Other Slug (PR #96553)** — fixed in 16.3.1-canary.0, ships in 16.3.1 stable; affects every app with `[...slug]` or `[...path]` routes on 16.3.0. **A new `## Next.js 16.3.0 STABLE — TypeScript 7 + Cache Components + Partial Prefetching + Cache Poisoning Fix (August 3, 2026)` section is appended to `patterns.md`** — covers all 5 patterns with full before/after code examples (TypeScript CLI opt-out for custom-transformer users, the canonical ISR + CC + PPF `getPost()` function, the cache-poisoning bug walkthrough + fix, the `instant` / `partialPrefetching` separation, the catch-all bug walkthrough + fix), practical-impact bullets for each, audit recipes for each, and a 4-bullet "Common mistakes" block at the end ("Next.js 16.3 is still in canary — no, it's stable", "leaving `useTypeScriptCli: true` in next.config.ts after upgrading", "using `instant` and expecting Partial Prefetching", "trusting catch-all routes on 16.3.0"). **(2) `security.md` was last touched 2026-07-29 (6 days stale)** and was missing the **three security-relevant changes** that ship in v16.3.0: **(a) Cache poisoning after prerender abort (PR #96426) — a correctness bug with security implications**: under `cacheComponents: true` + `'use cache'` + interactive navigation, an attacker could clear cached values via repeated fast navigation, then trick victims into seeing empty responses (empty shopping cart, empty notifications list, empty feed) for the `cacheLife()` duration; **(b) TypeScript CLI default-ON supply-chain implications (PR #96497)** — your installed `typescript` package is now on the build path; a compromised `typescript` package can now affect every build (not just editors); pin `typescript` explicitly; verify CI `engines.node` matches the TS 7 requirement (Node 20.15+ recommended); **(c) Catch-all index page hijack (PR #96553) — security implications**: requesting `/blog/anything` served the index page instead of the dynamic page; an information-disclosure vector (internal-route enumeration) + partial authorization-bypass potential (catch-all base serving less-restricted index while dynamic `[slug]` has stricter auth). **A new `## Next.js 16.3.0 STABLE — Cache Poisoning, Supply-Chain via TypeScript CLI Default, Catch-All Page Hijack (August 3, 2026)` section is appended to `security.md`** — covers all 3 with action items for each (bump + audit + defensive workaround), a "Forward-Looking" paragraph for the August 20, 2026 Vercel monthly security release (per the program launched July 13, 2026), and sources for each PR. **(3) `api.md` was last touched 2026-07-29 (6 days stale)** and was missing the **ISR + CC + PPF route handler pattern (PR #96526)** plus the cache-poisoning prevention pattern (PR #96426) plus the catch-all route handler bug fix (PR #96553). **A new `## ISR + Cache Components + Partial Prefetching Route Handler Pattern` section is appended to `api.md`** — covers the canonical 16.3.0 GET handler with `'use cache' + cacheLife('hours') + cacheTag` + the `RouteContext<'/api/posts/[id]'>` helper, a separate `revalidateTag` mutation endpoint with `'use server'` directive, the `next.config.ts` combo (`cacheComponents: true + experimental.partialPrefetching: true`), the 4-bullet "when to use" decision guide, and the 3-bullet "common mistakes" block (wrapping mutations in `'use cache'`, forgetting `cacheTag`, using `cacheLife('seconds')` on shell-eligible data). **A new `## Cache-Poisoning Prevention Pattern` section is appended to `api.md`** — shows the pre-16.3.0 buggy `getFeed()` pattern, the 16.3.0+ automatic fix, and the defensive try/catch workaround if you're stuck on pre-16.3.0. **A new `## Catch-All Route Handler Bug Fix` section is appended to `api.md`** — shows the pre-16.3.1-canary.0 bug, the audit recipe (`rg -l "\[\.\.\." app/api/` + the `curl` smoke test), and source links. **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.19** — react@latest still 19.2.8, react@canary still Flips `experimental.useTypeScriptCli` from default-`false` to default-`true` — every `next build` now runs the project-local `tsc` CLI by default (the path that already supports TypeScript 7's Go-native compiler). The diff is one line in `packages/next/src/server/config-shared.ts` (`useTypeScriptCli: false` → `useTypeScriptCli: true`); the docs page `useTypeScriptCli.mdx` is rewritten with the opt-in code example flipped to opt-out; `typescript.mdx` loses its entire opt-in section; `errors.json` adds 2 new error codes (1466 for "set to true" + 1467 for "set back to true"); the error-message wording in `runTypeScriptCli.ts` changes from "Enable" to "Set back to true" (reflecting that the default is now true). **Impact for every Next.js user**: (1) TypeScript 7 users (no flag needed — `tsc` is the Go-native binary by default) — just upgrade and **remove** the now-redundant `experimental: { useTypeScriptCli: true }` line from their config; (2) TypeScript 6 users — silent behavior change (CLI path adds ~50-200ms per build but uses the user's installed `tsc` formatter); (3) Custom transformers / `typescript` as a library users — `useTypeScriptCli` only affects `next build`'s type-check pass, so `require('typescript')` calls in tooling are independent, but if you want Next.js to keep using the JS Compiler API for compatibility, set `useTypeScriptCli: false` explicitly. **(b) PR #96308 by DavidIlie (`DavidIlie`, merged 2026-08-03T15:00:14Z, 2 files / +109/-8) — `Fix App Router scroll padding visibility`** — sticky-header scroll-to-element fix. Treats the root `scroll-padding-top` CSS property as the lower boundary of the usable viewport during App Router navigations, so content obscured by a sticky header is no longer incorrectly considered visible. The diff is a new `getScrollPaddingTopInPixels()` helper in `packages/next/src/client/components/layout-router.tsx` that resolves `scroll-padding-top` from `getComputedStyle` lazily (only for real scroll targets with client rects, so empty Fragments skip the lookup) + handles `px` / `%` units. **Impact for every App Router project with a sticky header** — set `html { scroll-padding-top: <header-height> }` in `globals.css` and bump to canary.108 (when published). Composes with PR #96342 (canary.103) which fixed the empty-Fragment scroll-ownership; the two fixes are orthogonal concerns. **(c) PR #93132 by icyJoseph (`icyJoseph`, merged 2026-08-03T16:21:06Z, 5 files / +67/-1) — `fix: double fragment on navigation`** — closes issues #93126 + #95551 (regression introduced in 16.2.0). The bug: navigating from `/abc#foo` to `/abc#bar` via `<Link href="/abc#bar">` left the URL at `/abc#foo#bar` (the segment cache stored the hashed canonical URL, so the new hash appended to the existing one). The fix: store a hashless canonical URL in the segment cache entry via `createHrefFromUrl(canonicalUrl, false)`. **Impact for every App Router project using `<Link>` for in-page anchor navigation** — TOC links, "back to top" links, accordion section anchors, skip-to-section links — all previously affected. Post-#93132, the URL bar shows the correct single hash. **5 NEW non-material PRs** (CI/test/docs only): PR #96386 [turbotrace] bump test version of sharp (styfle), PR #96527 [ci] Wait for the preview tarballs before running the deploy tests, PR #96526 docs: ISR with Cache Components and Partial Prefetching, PR #96534 test: skip hybrid not-found coverage for the legacy builder, PR #96505 Flag newly disabled deploy tests. **The canary.108 version-tag commit is not yet committed at this cron's check time** — expect canary.108 to npm-publish within hours on the 24h cadence once the tag lands. **(2) `typescript.md` had a same-day `experimental.useTypeScriptCli` documentation gap — the `Next.js 16.2.12 / 15.5.22 - TypeScript 7 Compatibility Matrix (July 25, 2026)` section documented `useTypeScriptCli` as an opt-in flag (the entire TS 7 compatibility story was "set `useTypeScriptCli: true` to enable TS 7 support"). The big news of this cycle: **`next@canary` canary-branch now has 3 NEW commits ahead of `16.3.0-canary.106`** (verified at 2026-08-03T12:02Z via `GET /repos/vercel/next.js/compare/v16.3.0-canary.106...canary` returning `ahead_by: 3`, up from `ahead_by: 2` at the v1.5.17 cron 12h ago). The v1.5.17 cron (Aug 3 00:03Z) said "canary-branch has 2 commits ahead of canary.106" — that is no longer true. **The 1 NEW commit since v1.5.17 is: PR #96426 by Janka Uryga (jankaeryga, Vercel Cache Components lead), merged 2026-08-03T11:42:26Z** — `[Cache] Make caches error if called after prerender aborts` — closes issue #96339. Caches that started filling *after* a prerender was aborted would silently produce an empty stream (because an aborted `renderSignal` made the `AbortSignal.any(...)` passed to `prerender()` inside `use-cache-wrapper` be aborted immediately — so the code produced an empty entry and saved it, essentially **poisoning the cache** with a fake empty result). The fix removes `renderSignal` from the `AbortSignal.any(...)` and short-circuits with a rejected promise *before* reaching the cache-fill codepath. The user-observable change: caches no longer silently poison themselves with empty entries when a prerender is aborted mid-fill. **Only affects apps with `cacheComponents: true`** that use `use cache` heavily under navigation aborts (fast-clicking links, slow connections, prefetch cancellations under `partialPrefetching: true`). **Will ship in `next@16.3.0-canary.107`** — but npm `dist-tag.canary` still points at canary.106 at this cron's check (canary.107 is built on the canary branch, version-tag commit `4fd843f` dated 2026-08-02T23:34:28Z, but NOT YET npm-published). The two earlier canary-branch-ahead-of-106 commits (PR #96493 by timneutkens on 2026-08-02T18:33:34Z + the canary.107 version tag `4fd843f`) are unchanged from v1.5.17 documentation. **Two weakest areas identified** — one headline, one routine: **(1) `performance.md` had a 12h-stale "## 16.3 canary.107-ahead" section** (last touched in v1.5.17 12h ago, documented 2 commits ahead of canary.106 with caveats like "PR #96493 + the canary.107 version-tag commit"). The 2 NEW commits are: **(1) PR #96493 by Tim Neutkens (timneutkens, Vercel co-founder/Next.js lead), merged 2026-08-02T18:33:34Z** — `Enable Turbopack build filesystem cache by default` — the **scope expansion of PR #96395 (sokra, canary.105)**. The canary.105 PR #96395 flipped `experimental.turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuild` to default-ON for `next build`, but only for the local + Vercel path (gated by a `turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuildDefault()` helper that returned `!isCI || Boolean(process.env.NOW_BUILDER)`). **PR #96493 removes that gate entirely** and defaults the option to `true` unconditionally — every `next build` in every environment (local, Vercel, GitHub Actions, GitLab, CircleCI, Buildkite, Jenkins, etc.) now uses the warm `.next/cache/turbopack/` filesystem cache by default. **Expected 5-30% warm-build speedup**, with 30-60% on Vercel-internal large apps. The diff is small (+22/-42 across 4 files) but decisive: `turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuildDefault()` function deleted, `turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuild: true` directly in `defaultConfig`, the JSDoc changed from "Defaults to `true` in canary/preview builds, `false` in production" to simply "Defaults to `true`". The docs page `turbopackFileSystemCache.mdx` is rewritten in the same commit — the "For deployment providers: Other providers can enable the build cache by default on their platform by setting `experimental.turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuild: true` from their adapter's `modifyConfig` hook" paragraph is **deleted entirely** because every provider is now treated the same as local. **The new responsibility on CI users**: opt out (`experimental.turbopackFileSystemCacheForBuild: false`) if your CI doesn't persist `.next/cache/` between runs. **(2) `4fd843f` — the `v16.3.0-canary.107` version-tag commit by `next-js-bot`**, dated 2026-08-02T23:34:28Z — NOT YET npm-published at this cron's check time (npm `dist-tag.canary` still points at `16.3.0-canary.106`); expected within hours on the 24h cadence. **Two weakest areas identified** — one headline, one routine: **(1) `performance.md` had a 12h-stale `canary.107-ahead` line in the `## 16.3 canary.106 SHIPPED` section** (last touched in v1.5.14, ~24h before this cron; v1.5.15 + v1.5.16 didn't touch it because the canary-branch ahead count was 0 in those cycles) that said "canary-branch is identical to canary.106" and "Expect canary.107 in 12-30h on the 24h cadence" — both observations are now stale. Projects on `@vinejs/vine@^4.x` were previously blocked — the resolver's old peer-dep cap would refuse to install alongside Vine 4 with `ERESOLVE` (or force-install with `--legacy-peer-deps`). 5.7.0 unblocks the v4 line. **(b) Schema-construction syntax alignment with Vine v4.2.0's new `vine.create({...})` shorthand** — replaces the previous `vine.compile(vine.object({...}))` pattern (one less level of wrapping per schema declaration). Test fixtures + README Quickstart example rewritten; the resolver core logic in `vine/src/vine.ts` is unchanged (only the type signature was tightened to `VineValidator<ConstructableSchema<Input, Output, Output>, any>` so the new `vine.create({...})` return type is accepted without TS narrowing complaints). The null-prototype-object guard for `Object.prototype` field-name handling from 5.5.8 / 5.6.0 carries over unchanged. **No new API, no new resolver helper, no new exports** — pure feature-coverage addition for the Vine subpath. **`forms.md` had zero mention of Vine/vineResolver at all** before this update (the resolver existed but was undocumented because the peer-dep cap meant most Vine v4 users couldn't even install it). **A new `## @hookform/resolvers 5.7.0 (August 2, 2026) — Vine v4 Resolver Support + \`vine.create({})\` Syntax` section is inserted right after the existing `## @hookform/resolvers 5.5.8 + 5.6.0` section** — covers both material changes in the 1-commit PR; the practical impact (Vine v4 users were blocked / Vine v2/v3 users see zero behavior change / Vine v2/v3 → v4 migrations get the new recommended syntax); a full before/after code example showing the `vine.compile(vine.object({...}))` → `vine.create({...})` migration in 5 lines; the Recommended Version Pin bumped from `^5.6.0` → `^5.7.0`; a 5-item migration checklist (pin bump + Vine v4 install + Vine v3 → v4 migration mechanical rewrite + "no migration required for >99% of forms" + drop `--legacy-peer-deps`); and a 4-link Sources block (release notes + PR #867 + commit `4cfba18` + VineJS v4.2.0 release notes). Both releases are about the same underlying behavior: the resolvers were silently dropping validation errors whose **root field-path** matched `Object.prototype` property names (`__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype`, `hasOwnProperty`, `toString`, `valueOf`, `length`, etc.) — a security hardening meant to prevent prototype pollution but applied too broadly. Forms using any of those names as a root-level schema key (`z.object({ __proto__: z.string() })`, `yup.object({ length: ... })`, `ajv` JSON Schema with `"properties": { "constructor": ... }`) were silently losing their validation errors. **A new `## @hookform/resolvers 5.5.8 + 5.6.0 (August 1, 2026) — Zod Resolver Drops Special Root Field Errors + All-Resolvers Generalization` section was inserted right after the existing `## @hookform/resolvers 5.5.4–5.5.7` section** — covers (a) 5.5.8's Zod-specific fix ([PR #869](https://github.com/react-hook-form/resolvers/issues/869), commit `2f28787`, published 2026-08-01T08:48:55Z) with the practical impact (config-style forms, CSS / DOM-keyed forms, form-library escape-hatch fields) + the audit recipe (`rg -n '(__proto__|constructor|prototype|hasOwnProperty|toString|valueOf):z.' schemas/ src/`); (b) 5.6.0's all-resolvers generalization (commit `b011a5f`, published 2026-08-01T09:13:32Z, ~25min after 5.5.8) with the per-resolver practical impact (Yup, AJV, Valibot, Joi, Superstruct, Typanion, Vest, Typebox) + the "why MINOR not PATCH" explanation (the new error-handling is the new contract for the whole package, not Zod-only); (c) a recommended version pin section bumping `^5.5.7` → `^5.6.0` with the migration checklist + a 4-link Sources block. The Common Mistakes section grows 2 new bullets — `@hookform/resolvers` 5.5.8 / 5.6.0 silently dropping validation errors on `Object.prototype` field names + relying on the silent-drop behavior as a "feature". **(3) TypeScript daily-cut no-content rebuild — `typescript@next` 7.1.0-dev.20260731.1 → 7.1.0-dev.20260801.1** (npm `dist-tag.next` moved 2026-08-01T08:14:09Z — TypeScript's daily rebuild at 08:25Z today shipped 8h after the v1.5.13 cron's check). Tenable Cloud Security tagged it as Plugin 445216 on 2026-07-23 (HIGH). v4.24.15 adds the same `@auth/core` patches plus v4-specific glue: explicit `NEXTAUTH_URL` precedence over auto-detected forwarded host (closes a redirect-host-injection vector behind trusted proxies), `uuid@^11.1.1` CommonJS pin (restores Node 18.x compatibility that the `uuid@14` ESM-only bump broke), and a one-time `OAuthSignInError` expected post-deploy from the state-cookie provider-binding change. **(2) `auth.md` had a Clerk coverage gap — `@clerk/nextjs` 7.5.12 (Jul 3, 2026) → 7.6.4 (Jul 31, 2026) SHIPPED 16 stable releases in 28 days, none of them documented in auth.md** (which only had a one-line table entry at line 12 and no dedicated `## Clerk` section at all). Tested against `vercel/front`. **(b) PR #96434** `Upgrade React from 0f42eac2-20260730 to cbb046ab-20260731` (vercel-release-bot, merged 2026-07-31T19:27:35Z) — the 6th React vendor bump in 11 days; brings React PR #37104 (`enableConditionalUseWarning` flag + `trackUsedThenable` fiber-parameter + `areSameKeyPath` helper + `hasPotentialUseWarnings()`/`clearUseWarnings()` exports) into Next.js's vendored React. **(c) PR #96437** `[turbopack] Introduce documentation on experimental.turbopackChunking` — docs PR for PR #96398's new config. **(d) PR #96428** `test: stabilize after() deploy revalidation checks` (test-only). **(e) PR #96438 + PR #96442 + PR #96359** — CI/test-infra changes. **`turbopackChunking` config is now GA in canary.105** — old `experimental.turbopack.chunkingHeuristics` + `experimental.turbopackGenerateComponentChunks` namespaces throw at config-eval time (per v1.5.10's documentation; the +9%/+8% build-time regression noted there is now live in npm). **(2) Next.js canary-branch now has **2 NEW commits ahead of canary.105** (canary-branch head `ccd47cfe53` at 2026-08-01T00:26:11Z)**: **(a) PR #96392** `Fix hybrid Pages/App Router not-found rendering with adapters` (ztanner, merged 2026-07-31T23:40:38Z, ~17 minutes **before** canary.105 was published — so the fix is canary-branch-ahead but the user-facing canary.105 didn't get it; users hitting this issue must wait for canary.106). The bug: when Pages + App Router routes coexist and a Pages route returns `notFound`, the handoff to App Router `/_not-found` failed in adapter-deployed environments — would attempt to resume the Pages entry which doesn't contain the relevant prerendered shell or postponed state. Fix marks the handoff with an empty postponed state, uses the existing resume protocol for a complete non-cacheable dynamic render. `next start` continues to use the normal PPR path. Trades PPR shell reuse for correctness only during this cross-router not-found handoff. Longer-term, adapters should have an explicit contract for cross-output handoffs so they can select and resume the App Router not-found output normally. Adds deployment regression coverage. **(b) PR #96448** `Warn that experimental.useCache is deprecated` (unstubbable, merged 2026-08-01T00:26:11Z). The `experimental.useCache` option has carried a `@deprecated` JSDoc annotation since PR #92316, but nothing surfaced that at runtime — users had no signal to migrate. This PR logs a warning whenever `experimental.useCache` is set explicitly, pointing at the top-level `cacheComponents` option. When `cacheComponents` is already enabled the option is redundant so the message says it can be removed; otherwise it asks the user to switch. **Disabling the option while `cacheComponents` is enabled is now rejected outright** — that combination is contradictory (turns off the very directive Cache Components is built around) and the resulting compile error asks the user to enable `cacheComponents` which they already have on. Throwing here matches how `cachedNavigations` and `partialPrefetching` reject configurations that require `cacheComponents`. The checks live in `assignDefaultsAndValidate` rather than alongside the other deprecations in `checkDeprecations`, because the latter runs before `enforceExperimentalFeatures` and would therefore not see a `cacheComponents` value that was enabled through the environment. A defined `experimental.useCache` value is what identifies an explicit user setting, since the option is otherwise backfilled from `cacheComponents` immediately below the new checks. **Expect canary.106 ship in the next 6-18h on the 24h cadence.** **(3) **React main branch has 2 NEW commits since the last canary cut** (cbb046ab-20260731 was the last cut, merged 2026-07-31T14:24:10Z; main-branch head is now `3a717e4243` at 2026-07-31T19:20:20Z with 2 NEW commits) — **forward-looking only, no canary cut yet, but expect a new `react@canary` cut within 12-48h on the React team's cadence**: **(a) PR #37063** `[Fiber] Collect Host Singleton children of Fragments` (eps1lon, merged 2026-07-31T19:20:21Z). Revisits the decision to exclude Host Singletons when collecting/traversing Fragment descendants for Fragment instance methods. Host Singletons were specifically excluded in react/react PR #32465 (discussion r1987663388). However, this leads to errors when calling `dispatchEvent` on empty singletons (e.g. `<body>`) as children of Fragment instances where `dispatchEvent` is called. From a type perspective, it's safe to start collecting `HostSingleton` in addition to `HostComponent` — both have an `Instance` in their `stateNode`. Should also be sound from a hierarchy perspective: React doesn't hoist these elements into a different place in the document as opposed to `HostHoistable` which would be moved to a different spot. **Practical impact: fixes a real bug where `dispatchEvent` calls on empty singletons inside Fragments were failing.** **(b) PR #37154** `[Flight] Add 'pending_weak' to Flight thenable protocol` (acdlite, merged 2026-07-31T17:22:31Z). Added behind a new experimental flag `enableFlightWeakThenables`. Adds a new thenable status to the Flight protocol: `'pending_weak'`. Unlike a regular pending thenable, a weak thenable does **not** block the stream from closing. If it settles while the stream is still open, its value is emitted like a normal pending thenable. Otherwise its reference is left unfulfilled and on the client it stays forever pending, **without erroring**, even when the connection closes. It's up to the client to handle the unresolved promise in an appropriate way. **Motivating use case** — encoding metadata about a Flight stream into the response itself. For example, a framework might want to track whether a page varies by search params. It could represent this in the response as a `Promise<boolean>` that resolves to `true` as soon as the component being rendered in the stream accesses search params. If the thenable never resolves by the time the stream closes, then the client knows that no search params were ever accessed. In the future a higher-level API could be added for encoding this kind of information; intentionally starting with the low-level primitive so frameworks can experiment in userspace without adding significantly to React's surface area. Internally Flight already uses its own private thenable statuses like `'resolved_model'`, and the protocol is designed to treat any status besides `'fulfilled'` and `'rejected'` as equivalent to `'pending'`, so `'pending_weak'` slots in cleanly. **Practical impact: zero observable change today (flag off). When the flag flips to ON in a future canary, framework authors can start encoding stream metadata via weak thenables.** **Tracked upstream versions updated**: **`next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.104 → The v1.5.07 cron captured `react@canary` = `1724e9ce-20260729` and stayed stable for 17h15min at the v1.5.07 cron start; the 4h42min-old bump was inside the v1.5.07 → v1.5.08 window. The new canary contains exactly **1 new commit: [React PR #37144](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/37144) `[Flight] Port ReplyServer traversal guards to FlightClient`** by Sebastian Silbermann (the same engineer who authored the recent Next.js PR #96085 `headers()` per-pass uniqueness fix), merged 2026-07-29T22:17:33Z. The fix is +26/-8 in a single file (`packages/react-client/src/ReactFlightClient.js`) — a two-spot defense-in-depth hardening of the Flight Client deserializer: (a) `getOutlinedModel` path-walk guard — checks `getPrototypeOf(value)` is `ObjectPrototype` or `ArrayPrototype` AND `hasOwnProperty.call(value, name)` before descending the path; throws `Error('Invalid reference.')` otherwise (blocks prototype-pollution via non-plain-prototype path entries); (b) `reviveModel` plain-object walk guard — wraps the `for (const k in value)` loop with `hasOwnProperty.call(value, k)`, so inherited enumerable properties from a poisoned prototype are skipped during reviver walks. The companion server-side `processReply` hardening already shipped earlier (the PR title explicitly says "Port ReplyServer traversal guards to FlightClient"). The Flight Client side was harder to harden because `getOutlinedModel`'s path-walk also does object-identity cache deduplication, and skipping the wrong reference would break the outline cache — so the guard is precisely scoped to fire only when the value is **non-plain**. Common trust model (your own server, your own RSC, your own database) is invisible; the guard matters for cross-tenant RSC caching, cross-origin RSC widgets, and `dangerouslyAllowBrowser: true` consumers. **No API change, no config flag, no codemod** — pure hardening. (2) **`@playwright/test@latest` 1.62.0 → 1.62.1** (npm `dist-tag.latest` moved 2026-07-30T16:36:55Z, GitHub release `v1.62.1` published at 2026-07-30T16:35:55Z) — exactly 6 days after 1.62.0 (Jul 24, 2026), missing from the v1.5.07 coverage. `rg "@playwright/test@latest" SKILL.md` still showed `1.62.0` at v1.5.07 commit time. Version bump 1.5.06 → 1.5.07. description: [30 Jul 2026 06:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **Next.js canary.103 SHIP-CONFIRMATION + postcss 8.5.23 + PWA guide serwist-examples cycle (canary.103 npm-published at 2026-07-30T00:11:44Z, ~8 minutes after v1.5.05 cron started — every PR v1.5.05 documented as "canary-branch ahead of canary.103" is now live in npm; canary-branch has had **zero new commits since canary.103 (verified via `GET /repos/vercel/next.js/compare/v16.3.0-canary.103...canary` returning 0 commits) — so this is purely a ship-confirmation + small-additions cycle, not a new gap-fill cycle). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **All 8 of the "canary-branch ahead of canary.103" PRs v1.5.05 documented are now SHIPPED in `16.3.0-canary.103`** — npm dist-tag moved at 2026-07-30T00:11:44Z (8min after v1.5.05 cron's 00:03Z start), GitHub release tag `v16.3.0-canary.103` published at 2026-07-30T00:04:37Z by `next-js-bot` (canary-branch head commit `f3edea1b06 — v16.3.0-canary.103`). The 19-PR list in the release body: PR #96360 PWA guide (Adham Fayrouz), **PR #96113 [fragment-scroll] Stop blurring on navigations** (Joseph — closes issue #96050), **PR #96364 Fix PPR rendering for configured HTML bots** (Zack Tanner), **PR #96107 Bump postcss to 8.5.23** (undocumented in v1.5.05!), **PR #96085 Ensure unique resolved `headers()` value between render passes** (Sebastian Silbermann), **PR #96325 Share Ecmascript HMR chunk versioning, diffing, and merging between browser and node** (wbinnssmith), **PR #96354 Make retry stop when the time it was given is up** (Playwright retry — time-based), **PR #96297 Gate partial fallback shell upgrades behind `partialPrefetching` for `next start`** (Andrew Clark), docs #96314 (modernize Next 16 upgrade AI guidance), **PR #96180 Cache immutable current task IDs outside the task-state lock** (Marcos Hernanz, Turbopack), **PR #95646 Added cache copying support for worktrees** (Jimmy Miller, Turbopack), docs #96146 (preventing-flash reconciling state), docs #96351 (Parallel routes, conditional rendering and auth), **PR #96319 [turbopack] Tree shake w/ `bar['foo']` syntax for star imports** (lukesandberg, was the only material commit ahead of canary.102 — now confirmed shipped in canary.103), **PR #96299 Update skills for Partial Prefetching** (undocumented in v1.5.05! — minor skills maintenance for the Next.js repo's own AI agent skills to handle Partial Prefetching changes), docs #96316 (remove PPR adapter page from Pages Router), CI #96195 (build_reusable background steps), **PR #96321 Bump @types/node to 20.17.7 to fix findSourceMap type** (dan), docs #96324 (Restore comments dropped during a refactor). All 8 material PRs that v1.5.05 documented as "canary-branch ahead of canary.103 / expected in `16.3.0-canary.103`" have landed — the caveat text in those sections is now obsolete and the "expected in `16.3.0-canary.103`" framing has been replaced with **"SHIPPED in `16.3.0-canary.103`"** throughout. (2) **Two canary.103 PRs that were missed by v1.5.05** (one bundled-dep bump + one docs-only PWA guide enhancement, both small but worth recording for completeness): **(a) PR #96107 `Bump postcss to 8.5.23`** — a 1-commit bundled-dep bump, `postcss` 8.5.22 → 8.5.23. PostCSS 8.5.23 is a recent security/bugfix train (released upstream late July 2026, no CVE — mostly CSS parser crash recovery for malformed `@layer` declarations + an idempotent-reformat fix for `postcss-custom-properties` fallback values). For Next.js users this is transparent (Next.js bundles its own `postcss-loader` chain), but anyone using a custom `postcss.config.cjs` that runs untrusted CSS (CMS-generated CSS, user-uploaded theme files, dynamic `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` style strings) gets the better parser robustness for free on canary.103+. **(b) PR #96360 `Update PWA guide with enhanced offline support details and example links for serwist`** by Adham Fayrouz — docs-only update to `/docs/app/guides/progressive-web-apps`, adds serwist example links (specifically the [`serwist/examples/next-basic`](https://github.com/serwist/serwist/tree/main/examples/next-basic) repo and a note that the Serwist plugin currently requires webpack configuration — important for Turbopack users). Worth a brief mention in `setup.md` since the PWA guide is the canonical place users discover offline support, and the Serwist-vs-Next-bundled-`experimental.useOffline` distinction is now more clearly articulated in the docs (Serwist for webpack-based projects that want full custom-service-worker control; `experimental.useOffline` for Turbopack users who want automatic offline retry without a custom worker). **Tracked upstream versions updated**: **`next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.102 → 16.3.0-canary.103** (npm `dist-tag.canary` moved 2026-07-30T00:11:44Z, GitHub release tag published at 2026-07-30T00:04:37Z; 19 commits vs canary.102 — 8 NEW material PRs documented in v1.5.05 + 2 NEW material PRs documented in v1.5.06 [PR #96107 postcss + PR #96360 PWA guide] + 1 NEW minor skills PR [PR #96299]). **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.05** (next@latest still 16.2.12, next@backport still 15.5.22, next@preview still 16.3.0-preview.10, react@latest still 19.2.8, react@canary still 19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729, typescript@latest still 7.0.2, typescript@next still 7.1.0-dev.20260728.1, vite@latest still 8.1.5, vitest@latest still 4.1.10, vitest@beta still 5.0.0-beta.7, @biomejs/biome still 2.5.6, tailwindcss@latest still 4.3.3, better-auth@latest still 1.6.25, shadcn@latest still 4.16.0, @playwright/test@latest still 1.62.0, @tanstack/react-query@latest still 5.101.4, zustand@latest still 5.0.14). **Changes**: performance.md (the `## 16.3 canary.103-ahead Turbopack HMR Sharing + PPR HTML-Bots Fix + Worktree Cache Copying + turbo-tasks Lock-Free Reads (July 29, 2026, canary-branch ahead of canary.103)` section header rewritten to `## 16.3 canary.103 SHIPPED — Turbopack HMR Sharing + PPR HTML-Bots Fix + Worktree Cache Copying + turbo-tasks Lock-Free Reads (July 30, 2026)`, the opening paragraph replaced to confirm the ship event with the npm publish timestamp + GitHub release timestamp + the 0-commits-on-canary-branch-since-canary.103 observation, all 5 subsection headers + their source-block labels updated from "canary-branch ahead of canary.103" to "**SHIPPED in `16.3.0-canary.103`**" + "expected in `16.3.0-canary.103`" → "**SHIPPED in `16.3.0-canary.103`**", and 2 new subsections appended after the existing 5 — `### Bundled-Dependency Update — postcss 8.5.23 (PR #96107, SHIPPED in canary.103, July 30, 2026)` covering the bundled-dep bump with the practical-impact note for custom postcss.config.cjs users + the transparent-default-for-Next-users note + a 1-link Source; `### PWA Guide Enhanced With Serwist Example Links (PR #96360, SHIPPED in canary.103, July 30, 2026)` covering the docs-only guide enhancement with the Serwist-vs-`experimental.useOffline` distinction + a 2-link Sources block); Version bump 1.5.05 → 1.5.06. description: 
[30 Jul 2026 00:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **React canary bump + canary-branch-ahead cycle (react@canary shipped 46 minutes after the v1.5.04 cron started, and Next.js canary.103 is staged but not yet published). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **`react@canary` advanced `19.3.0-canary-96fcba90-20260728` → `19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729`** at 2026-07-29T18:48:34Z — 46 minutes AFTER the v1.5.04 cron's 18:03Z start time and BEFORE the next Next.js vendor-bump PR will pick it up. The previous cron completely missed this React canary (only 2 commits, but both materially important). The headline is **[React PR #37135](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/37135) `[Fiber] Fix hang when updating a dehydrated boundary inside a hidden tree`** by dan — **closes Next.js issue #95848** ("Blank page when pressing Back between a reload and hydration with cacheComponents (regression in 16.3.0-canary.87)"), which was the entire reason **Next.js PR #95682** (`Replay same-document traversals that happen before hydration`) **got reverted in PR #95853** on 2026-07-15. The replacement PR **[#96252](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/96252)** is open and explicitly waits for this React fix — so the **next Next.js canary after canary.103** (likely canary.104 or canary.105) will be able to un-revert #95682, restoring the "press Back during hydration, router replays the missed navigation" behavior. The second commit **[React PR #37125](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/37125) `[DOM] Blur focused descendants in Fragment refs`** by Minh Vu is a smaller fix — `FragmentInstance.blur()` now matches nested focused elements (previously only the first level of host children), fixing a silent focus-leak in Fragment refs. No public API or config changes. (2) **Next.js canary-branch has 19 commits ahead of canary.103 (which is staged but not yet published — npm `dist-tag.next` still points to canary.102)** — and 8 of those commits are materially user-facing (most of the rest are docs, CI, and tests). The most material: **PR #96113 [fragment-scroll] Stop blurring on navigations** (Joseph, merged 2026-07-29T23:13:36Z — closes Next.js issue #96050, fixes the regression introduced in canary.71 where query-string-only `router.push/replace` blurs the focused input and dismisses the mobile keyboard; affects every form-heavy App Router app — search-as-you-type, filter inputs synced to URL); **PR #96364 Fix PPR rendering for configured HTML bots** (Zack Tanner, merged 2026-07-29T22:49:58Z — when `cacheComponents` was on, requests matching a custom `htmlLimitedBots` pattern bypassed PPR; the fix routes them through the existing streaming-metadata decision so the configured blocking-metadata behavior is now applied consistently — relevant for any project with custom SEO bot user-agent rules); **PR #96325 Share Ecmascript HMR chunk versioning, diffing, and merging between browser and node** (wbinnssmith, merged 2026-07-29T21:51:34Z — Turbopack: the `chunk_list/merged_update.rs` stack now lives in `turbopack-ecmascript/src/hmr/` (`version.rs`, `update.rs`, `content.rs`, `merger.rs`) and both browser and node runtimes share it via a `value_trait` on `EcmascriptHmrChunkContent` — eliminates the duplicated copy of the HMR machinery, fixes a copy-paste "chunk path ... is not in client root" error string that should have read "output root", and now applies node's lazy `entries()` materialization to the browser path so chunks with only deletions don't materialize any `Vc<Code>`; the unification of browser+node chunk-content structs is explicitly out of scope — needs `Box<dyn ChunkingContext>` widening because both chunking contexts define `minify_type` twice); **PR #96085 Ensure unique resolved `headers()` value between render passes** (Sebastian "Sebbie" Silbermann, merged 2026-07-29T22:16:35Z — a single HTTP request can render the same React tree in multiple passes with different semantics for `connection()` (the prospective and final prerenders of a runtime prefetch, or a navigation's dynamic render + the runtime prerender spawned from it to refresh the client's prefetch cache), and all of those passes previously resolved `headers()` to the same sealed headers object — so userland caches keyed on the identity of `await headers()` leaked promises across passes; the fix gives every render pass a distinct object over the same underlying data, making "the headers object identifies the request within one render pass" a real contract); **PR #96297 Gate partial fallback shell upgrades behind `partialPrefetching` for `next start`** (Andrew Clark, merged 2026-07-29T19:56:50Z — PR #96074 put `partialFallback` behind `partialPrefetching` for the deploy adapter but missed the `next start` path, which performs the upgrade itself in the compiled page runtime; this PR gates only the *upgrade* (the background ISR revalidation that specializes a shell per request + the client-facing `isFallbackUpgradeable` signal) — not the shell machinery as a whole — so build-time partial prerendering of params and serving build-time sub-shells continue working regardless of `partialPrefetching`); **PR #96180 Cache immutable current task IDs outside the task-state lock** (Marcos Hernanz, merged 2026-07-29T19:22:34Z — Turbopack `turbo-tasks`: introduces a cloneable `CurrentTaskStateHandle` that keeps the execution's immutable `Option<TaskId>` beside the existing shared `Arc<RwLock<CurrentTaskState>>`; `current_task_if_available` and `current_task` no longer take a shared `RwLock` on every native function call — the lock and its contention path remained visible in both HMR and production profiles; the absolute percentage is small (1–2% range, the PR's own evidence is directional) but it's also source-independent and applies to every execution); **PR #95646 Added cache copying support for worktrees** (Jimmy Miller, merged 2026-07-29T18:52:36Z — Turbopack now detects if it is in a worktree, finds the main repo, and copies turbopack caches for build or dev to make initial build faster; the implementation only looks at the root project (not other worktrees) — important for any monorepo where engineers use `git worktree add` to parallelize branches); **PR #96321 Bump @types/node to 20.17.7 to fix findSourceMap type** (dan, merged — fixes a type error that was silently failing in some Node.js 20.x types). Plus **PR #96319 (Turbopack star-import string-key tree-shaking, already documented in v1.5.04)** — the only material commit ahead of canary.102; everything else ahead of canary.103 is docs (#96316 PPR adapter page removal, #96351 parallel routes auth, #96146 preventing-flash reconciling state, #96314 Next 16 upgrade AI guidance, #96360 PWA guide offline links), CI (#96195 build_reusable background), comment-restoration (#96324), and Turbopack internal commits. **Tracked upstream versions updated**: **`react@canary` 19.3.0-canary-96fcba90-20260728 → 19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729** (npm `dist-tag.canary` moved 2026-07-29T18:48:34Z, `dist-tag.experimental` moved in lockstep to `0.0.0-experimental-1724e9ce-20260729` at 2026-07-29T18:47:43Z; 2 commits between the two, both bug fixes — no new APIs, no config flags). **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.04** (next@latest still 16.2.12, next@canary still 16.3.0-canary.102 because canary.103 was tagged in the canary-branch commit `f3edea1` at 2026-07-29T23:50:00Z but hasn't been npm-published yet — expect the publish within the next 2-6 hours based on the 24h cadence, at which point the canary dist-tag will move to canary.103 and the 8 material PRs below will start showing up in your `pnpm-lock.yaml`), next@preview still 16.3.0-preview.10, react@latest still 19.2.8, typescript@latest still 7.0.2, typescript@next still 7.1.0-dev.20260728.1, vite@latest still 8.1.5, vitest@latest still 4.1.10, vitest@beta still 5.0.0-beta.7, @biomejs/biome still 2.5.6, tailwindcss@latest still 4.3.3, better-auth@latest still 1.6.25, shadcn@latest still 4.16.0, @playwright/test@latest still 1.62.0, @tanstack/react-query@latest still 5.101.4, zustand@latest still 5.0.14. Vite published a new **8.2.0-beta.0** pre-release on 2026-07-15 (not tracked on `@latest` — included only as a forward-looking note). **Changes**: components.md (new `## React 19.3.0-canary-1724e9ce-20260729 — [Fiber] Hidden-Tree Dehydration Hang Fix (#37135) + Fragment-Ref Blur Nesting Fix (#37125) (July 29, 2026)` section appended after the existing `## React 19.3.0-canary-28cd4bb0-20260723` section — covers the Next.js issue #95848 / PR #95682 / PR #96252 chain in detail, the two PRs themselves, the Next.js canary vendor bump expectation, the timing analysis showing this React canary shipped 46min after the v1.5.04 cron and is therefore already missed-by-design for that cron, the React Canary Coverage table updated, and 10-link Sources block); performance.md (new `## 16.3 canary.103-ahead Turbopack HMR Sharing + PPR HTML-Bots Fix + Worktree Cache Copying + turbo-tasks Lock-Free Reads (July 29, 2026, canary-branch ahead of canary.103)` section appended after the existing `## 16.3 canary.100–102 Server HMR Refactor + Tree-Shaking Extensions + Dropped-Dynamic-Import Build Fix` section — 5 subsections covering PR #96325 (Turbopack Ecmascript HMR chunk versioning/diffing/merging sharing) with the file-level breakdown + the copy-paste error string fix + the "no new ChunkingContext methods" caveat + the chunk-content-struct unification explicitly out-of-scope note + a 5-line ripgrep audit recipe for custom Turbopack plugins; PR #96364 (Fix PPR rendering for configured HTML bots) with the streaming-metadata-decision reuse note + the test file path + the "no new config" note; PR #96180 (Cache immutable current task IDs) with the `CurrentTaskStateHandle` shape + the 1-2% directional perf note + the source-independent rationale + the audit note that this is a Turbopack internal change with no user-facing API; PR #96297 (Gate partial fallback shell upgrades behind `partialPrefetching` for `next start`) with the "only the upgrade, not the shell machinery" caveat + the deploy-adapter vs `next start` divergence explanation + the new fixture test path; PR #95646 (Cache copying support for worktrees) with the worktree-detection recipe + the "root project only, not other worktrees" caveat + the git-requirement note); routing.md (new `## Query-String `router.push`/`replace` No Longer Blurs Focused Input — PR #96113 (canary-branch ahead of canary.103, July 29, 2026)` section appended after the existing `## 16.3 canary.87–96 Routing, Navigation & Middleware Fixes (July 17–24, 2026)` section — covers the regression introduced in canary.71's new scroll/focus handler (`appNewScrollHandler`), the iOS Safari / Android Chrome software-keyboard dismissal that breaks search-as-you-type, the before/after `document.activeElement` behavior, the e2e test that ships in the PR, the "attempts a fix that prevents blur" open checkbox on the PR (the PR description explicitly notes a full fix that prevents the blur is still TODO — this PR just adds the e2e coverage to lock in the existing `query-string-only` behaviour and remove the regression for that specific path), the auditable audit recipe for any App Router codebase using query-string routing, and a 4-link Sources block); server-components.md (new `## `headers()` Returns Unique Object Per Render Pass — PR #96085 (canary-branch ahead of canary.103, July 29, 2026)` section appended near the existing headers()/cookies() documentation — covers the per-pass uniqueness contract, the leaky-promise failure mode for userland caches keyed on headers identity, the 3-pass scenarios (prospective + final prerender, dynamic + runtime spawn), the recommended pattern of using `headers()` as a request-marker only within one pass and re-resolving per pass, and a 3-link Sources block); testing.md (new `## Playwright `retry` Now Respects the Given Duration — PR #96354 (canary-branch ahead of canary.103, July 29, 2026)` section appended after the existing `## Playwright 1.62 (July 24, 2026)` section — covers the bug where `retry(fn, 15_000)` would actually run for ~3 minutes (15000ms / 500ms × the per-try stall, which for browser selectors is 5s × 31 tries = 170103ms), the new time-based accounting, the "always makes at least one try + the interval no longer has to divide the duration" semantics, the "last try is slow → still waited for that try to end" caveat, the dev-loop implication for agents that lose an hour diagnosing "Exceeded timeout of 120000 ms" with no underlying assertion visible, and a 3-link Sources block); SKILL.md (description prepend + version 1.5.04 → 1.5.05 + 1 tracked React version bump + canary-branch PR summary inline). Version bump 1.5.04 → 1.5.05. description: 
[29 Jul 2026 18:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **security.md + performance.md gap-fill cycle (Node.js July 27 security release was POSTPONED twice and PUBLISHED on Wednesday July 29, 2026 — 18 hours after the previous cron captured the pre-announcement but before it shipped). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **`security.md` was 18 hours stale on the Node.js security release** — the v1.5.03 cron (Jul 29 00:03Z) only had the embargoed pre-announcement (HIGH ceiling + expected Node 22.24.x / 24.19.x / 26.4.x). The actual release went out Wed Jul 29 with **9 CVEs** (3 HIGH, 5 MEDIUM, 3 LOW) — patch versions **Node 22.23.2**, **Node 24.18.1**, **Node 26.5.1** — plus `undici` 8.9.0 / 7.29.0 / 6.28.0 + `llhttp` 9.4.3 dependency updates. The 3 HIGH CVEs: **CVE-2026-56846** HTTP/2 retained-headers maxSessionMemory bypass (remote memory-exhaustion DoS, 24.x + 22.x), **CVE-2026-56848** HTTP/2 re-entrant `nghttp2_session_mem_send` heap-use-after-free (26.x + 24.x + 22.x — the most operationally serious of the three, memory-safety class that frequently gets RCE’d), **CVE-2026-58043** Permission Model path-matching over-grant across radix-tree prefix boundaries (22.x + 24.x + 26.x — the first Permission Model HIGH of 2026, a security-boundary bypass not a DoS). The 5 MEDIUM: CVE-2026-56850 HTTPS Agent mTLS identity reuse across PFX certs, CVE-2026-58040 HTTPS Agent hostname-verification skip in TLS session reuse (incomplete fix for CVE-2026-48934), CVE-2026-58041 node:sqlite SQLTagStore iterator write replay, CVE-2026-58042 dns.resolveAny() abort on >256 A records, CVE-2026-58045 node:zlib sync spoofed-TypedArray crash. The 3 LOW: CVE-2026-56847 Permission Model trace_events filesystem bypass, CVE-2026-58039 Permission Model process.report filesystem bypass, CVE-2026-58044 HTTP parser header-truncation request smuggling. **The two-day postpone (Mon Jul 27 → Tue Jul 28 → Wed Jul 29) is unusual** — nodejs.org cites "additional testing and validation" then "infrastructure issues" — so future pre-announcement windows should leave a 3-day buffer. (2) **Turbopack star-import string-key tree-shaking (PR #96319)** — the only material canary-branch commit ahead of canary.102 (most others are docs + CI). Adds a 6-line gate in `turbopack-ecmascript/src/analyzer/imports.rs` that recognizes the `MemberProp::Computed(ComputedPropName { expr: box Expr::Lit(Lit::Str(_)), .. })` form (e.g. `ns["foo"]`) and narrows the usage to just the literal key, instead of expanding to `All` (which defeats tree-shaking). Small but real barrel-pruning improvement for projects using the dynamic-key string-literal pattern (icon libraries, jQuery-style plugin registries, lodash-style utilities indexed by name). **Tracked upstream versions unchanged from 1.5.03**: `next@latest` 16.2.12, `next@backport` 15.5.22, `next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.102, `next@preview` 16.3.0-preview.10, `react@latest` 19.2.8, `react@canary` 19.3.0-canary-96fcba90-20260728, `typescript@latest` 7.0.2, `typescript@next` 7.1.0-dev.20260728.1, `vite@latest` 8.1.5, `vitest@latest` 4.1.10, `vitest@beta` 5.0.0-beta.7, `@biomejs/biome` 2.5.6, `tailwindcss@latest` 4.3.3, `better-auth@latest` 1.6.25, `shadcn@latest` 4.16.0, `@playwright/test@latest` 1.62.0, `@tanstack/react-query@latest` 5.101.4, `zustand@latest` 5.0.14. **Changes**: security.md (the existing `## Node.js July 27, 2026 Security Release — Patch Day (HIGH in Each Line)` section is **completely rewritten** with the actual POST-release content — section header renamed to `## Node.js July 29, 2026 Security Release — 9 CVEs / 3 HIGH (Originally July 27, Delayed Twice)`, the pre-announcement-only "What's Confirmed (pre-release window)" table replaced with a full 10-row "Release Versions & Affected Lines" table, three new subsections added — 9-row "CVE List — 9 Total (3 HIGH, 5 MEDIUM, 3 LOW)" table with explicit per-CVE affected lines + reporter/fixer + impact, 2-row "Bundled-Dependency Updates" table (`undici` 8.9.0/7.29.0/6.28.0 + `llhttp` 9.4.3), 6-step "Post-Patch Verification Recipe" bash guide with `process.versions.undici` / `llhttp` / `nghttp2` / `openssl` printed-version checks + a Permission Model regression test for CVE-2026-58043; the 7-step "Pre-Patch Audit Recipe" is preserved (now titled "Pre-Patch Audit Recipe (Run Before You Bump)" — same playbook as the June 17 release); the "What HIGH Usually Means" section is rewritten to "What HIGH Actually Means in This Release" with the historical-pattern check (2 HIGH in HTTP/2 + 1 HIGH in Permission Model matches the 2026 crypto/TLS/HTTP trend, Permission Model HIGH is the first of 2026); Version bump 1.5.03 → 1.5.04. description: "[29 Jul 2026 00:03Z] Routine 6h cycle — **setup.md + performance.md + api.md gap-fill cycle (Next.js canary.100→102 SHIPPED 8 hours before this cron — a 3-release cluster within 3 hours that the previous cron missed). Two weakest areas identified**: (1) **Turbopack `import.meta.env` support (PR #96225 by timneutkens, SHIPPED in 16.3.0-canary.100 at 2026-07-28T21:04:54Z)** — the single biggest new user-facing feature in this cycle, completely missing from the skill. `rg -i "import.meta.env" *.md` audit returned ZERO matches across all 17 skill files. Turbopack now has native first-class support for `import.meta.env.DEV` / `.PROD` / `.MODE` / `.BASE_URL` / `.SSR` — Vite-compatible API for build-mode constants. Until this PR, Turbopack only supported `process.env.X`; `import.meta.env.X` was undefined, forcing Vite-migrated codebases to rewrite every `import.meta.env.X` to `process.env.X`. That rewrite is **no longer required**. The 5 built-ins are statically analyzed and replaced at build time (DCE-friendly), with `BASE_URL` derived from `basePath` + Vite-compat trailing slash, and `SSR` as a new Turbopack-specific constant (true server-side, false in client bundle). (2) **Turbopack server HMR refactor cluster (PR #94948 + #95661 + #95795 + #95546, all by wbinnssmith, SHIPPED in 16.3.0-canary.101 at 2026-07-28T21:13:44Z)** — a 4-PR refactor that replaces per-chunk server HMR turbo tasks with a single firehose subscription that diffs every HMR chunk together. **Multi-second saving on both cold and warm builds in large apps.** `clear()` is renamed to `reEvaluateAllModulesExpensive()` to label directly that this is a costly operation. `Performance.md` had zero coverage of any of these. **Tracked upstream versions updated**: `next@canary` 16.3.0-canary.99 → **16.3.0-canary.102** (npm `dist-tag.next` moved 3 times in this 6h window: canary.100 at 2026-07-28T21:04:54Z, canary.101 at 2026-07-28T21:13:44Z, canary.102 at 2026-07-28T23:55:12Z; current 1.2.2 published 2026-07-26). 5.7.1 is a **single-commit PATCH release** — `fix: ata-validator peer dependency version` ([issue #871](https://github.com/react-hook-form/resolvers/issues/871), commit [`827f20b`](https://github.com/react-hook-form/resolvers/commit/827f20bd97a4618d75162e54fe9ba2419fd4e50a), merged 2026-08-02T06:08:14Z, 3 files / +18/-12 lines) — bumping the peer-dep in `@hookform/resolvers/ata-validator/package.json` + the workspace `bun.lock`. **No new resolver, no new helper, no source-code change in any subpath module** — the only diff is the peer-dep string + the lockfile. **Three practical-impact cases**: **(a) Anyone on `ata-validator@^1.x`** (the majority of new installs since 1.0.0 shipped Jul 15) — was blocked from installing `@hookform/resolvers/ata-validator` because npm's peer-dep resolver sees `^0.7.0` as a narrower range than the already-installed `^1.2.0` (no `0.x` overlap with `1.x`), so `npm install` failed with `ERESOLVE` or installed with `--legacy-peer-deps`. **5.7.1 unblocks the install.** **(b) Anyone still on `ata-validator@^0.7.x`** is unaffected by the *content* of the change (the resolver still works against the `^0.7.x` API surface), but the new `^1.2.0` peer cap means upgrading `@hookform/resolvers` to 5.7.1 without bumping `ata-validator` first will trigger a *new* `ERESOLVE` (the reverse-direction failure). Safe path: `npm install ata-validator@^1.2.0 @hookform/resolvers@^5.7.1` together, or stay on 5.7.0 if committed to the 0.x line. **(c) Anyone not using `@hookform/resolvers/ata-validator`** (the >99.9% case — the `ata-validator` library is a niche alternative validator to Zod/Yup/Valibot) — has zero behavior change. The peer-dep string lives only in that subpath's `package.json` — the rest of the package's 30+ resolvers are unaffected. **A new `## @hookform/resolvers 5.7.1 (August 2, 2026) — \`ata-validator\` Subpath Peer-Dep Bump \`^0.7.0\` → \`^1.2.0\`` section is inserted right after the existing `## @hookform/resolvers 5.7.0` section** — covers the full before/after peer-dep diff, the 6-row platform-native companion table (`@ata-validator/native-darwin-arm64` + 5 more for linux-arm64/linux-x64/win32-x64 with gnu/musl variants), the 3-bullet practical impact (unblocks 1.x users / forces 0.7.x users to migrate in one step / zero change for non-ata-validator users), the Recommended Version Pin bumped from `^5.7.0` → `^5.7.1` with the "Pin moved up 13 minutes after v1.5.15 set it" annotation, a 5-item migration checklist, a 3-recipe audit block (`rg -n "from '@hookform/resolvers/ata-validator'"` to confirm subpath usage + `npm ls ata-validator` to confirm version + `cat node_modules/.../package.json | jq .peerDependencies` to confirm the installed peer string), and a 5-link Sources block (v5.7.1 release notes + issue #871 + commit `827f20b` + ata-validator npm page + `@ata-validator/native-darwin-arm64` npm page). The Common Mistakes section grows 1 new bullet — `@hookform/resolvers` 5.7.1: missing ata-validator 1.x peer-dep — with the same audit recipe as the new section. **All other tracked upstream versions unchanged from v1.5.15** — specifically: next@latest still 16.2.12, next@backport still 15.5.22, next@canary still 16.3.0-canary.106 (canary-branch still 0 commits ahead — unchanged from v1.5.15; the v1.5.15 prediction that canary.107 would arrive 6-24h from then is still open at this cron's 6h-after mark, so canary.107 is expected 6-24h from now), next@preview still 16.3.0-preview.10, react@latest still 19.2.8, react@canary still 19.3.0-canary-cbb046ab-20260731 (npm dist-tag stable for 43h11min at cron start — React main branch still has the 2 NEW commits from v1.5.12 documented: PR #37063 [Fiber Collect Host Singleton children of Fragments] + PR #37154 [Flight pending_weak]; 0 new commits in the past 24h window, so the forward-looking window for the next React canary cut is now 0-36h on the React team's cadence), typescript@latest still 7.0.2, typescript@next still 7.1.0-dev.20260802.1 (npm `dist-tag.next` moved 2026-08-02T08:25:51Z — **8th consecutive no-content daily rebuild**; ## Critical Rules (Never Forget)

- **`'use client'` boundary** — Only add when you need browser APIs, hooks, or interactivity. Keep everything else server-side.
- **Server Components can't use hooks** — No `useState`, `useEffect`, `useContext` in server components.
- **Colocate data fetching** — Fetch data in the component that needs it, not prop-drill from the top.
- **Zod for all schema validation** — Never trust raw form/API data without validation.
- **Tailwind = utility-first** — Don't fight it. Compose utilities, don't fight the cascade.
- **Server Actions = mutations** — Use them for form submits and data mutations; fetch for reads.
- **`<Image>` over `<img>`** — Always use Next's Image for automatic optimization and CLS prevention.
- **Abort in-flight requests** — Use `AbortController` to prevent race conditions on navigation.
- **Types in, types out** — Mirror backend response types exactly. No `any` from API calls.

## Version Defaults

^- **Next.js 16.2.10** (latest stable — App Router, Server Components, Server Actions, Turbopack stable for production, Node.js proxy, `use cache` directive, PPR stable; **16.3.0-canary.72** released June 30, 2026 ([tag cut 2026-06-30T23:41:13Z, npm `dist-tag.next` pointer moved 2026-07-01T00:05:02Z](https://www.npmjs.com/package/next) — 22 commits since [canary.71](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/compare/v16.3.0-canary.71...v16.3.0-canary.72); the **17 in-flight material PRs previously documented as `will be in canary.72+`** are now officially shipped). **16.3.0-canary.73 SHIPPED (July 1, 2026 — npm `canary` dist-tag pointer moved 2026-07-01T16:35Z, tag cut 2026-07-01T16:33:19Z, branch HEAD `c5645f69`, 17 commits since canary.72 — arrived 6–7 hours earlier than the 24h cadence predicted):** the 5 in-flight commits from the previous cron's coverage all shipped. Full canary.72 → canary.73 PR-by-PR diff at [compare/v16.3.0-canary.72...v16.3.0-canary.73](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/compare/v16.3.0-canary.72...v16.3.0-canary.73). FIVE new material PRs landed on the canary branch after the 12:04 UTC coverage and are now officially in canary.73: **(L) [PR #95151](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95151) — `[PP] Validate Shell prefetches (except gSP)`** by Janka Uryga, merged 2026-07-01T04:36:41Z — instant validation for `partialPrefetching: true` (or per-segment `prefetch = 'partial'` / `'unstable_eager'`) routes, where `<Link>` prefetches an App Shell that cannot access link data. New `DynamicHoleKind.Link = 1` in `packages/next/src/server/app-render/dynamic-rendering.ts` (renumbers `Runtime` 1→2 and `Dynamic` 2→3); new stage `ShellRuntime` added between `Static` and `Runtime` in `instant-validation.tsx`'s `SEGMENT_STAGE_ORDER`; new error builders `createLinkBodyErrorInNavigation` (error 1390), `createLinkMetadataError` (error 1391), `createLinkViewportError` (error 1392) in `packages/next/src/server/app-render/blocking-route-messages.ts`; new entries in `packages/next/errors.json`. Detection: if a hole is present in `ShellRuntime` but disappears in `Runtime`, it's link data (link data can resolve in Runtime but not in App Shell). 21 new test fixtures in `test/e2e/app-dir/instant-validation/app/shells/(default)/` (8 valid + 6 invalid route combos × instant config matrix); plus new `valid-session-only` / `valid-session-with-dynamic` fixtures prove session data is allowed outside Suspense in App Shells. **Known limitation (author-noted):** uses chunks from the dev render that resolve static params in Static stage, so static params are incorrectly included in ShellRuntime and don't trigger link-data errors — fixed by follow-up PR #94595. **(M) [PR #94595](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/94595) — `[PP] Instant validation - error for unguarded static params`** by Janka Uryga, merged 2026-07-01T06:59:43Z — follow-up to #95151 extending link-data detection to `generateStaticParams`. Static params resolve in `Static` (for HTML-shell validation, initial-load path) vs `ShellRuntime` (for client-nav on `partialPrefetching: true`) via the new `requestStore.needsSessionShell` flag (DEV-only, set in three places in `app-render.tsx`). Three params utilities moved from inline definitions in `params.ts` into new `packages/next/src/server/lib/params-utils.ts` (`isEmptyParams`, `hasFallbackRouteParams`, `allParamsAreRootParams` + new `hasNonRootStaticParams`); `app-render.tsx` calls `hasNonRootStaticParams` to decide whether to skip the secondary render (optimization when no static params exist). **Known limitation (author-noted):** pre-existing `fallbackParams` bug in build validation (two tests marked `// TODO(app-shells): missing fallback params in build validation`); also doesn't touch `environmentName` (deliberately, follow-up PR coming). **(N) [#95336](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95336)** revert of test runner migration** — CI only, no user-facing impact. **(O) [#95106](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95106)** refactor: split out validateStaticShell** — internal refactor. **(P) [#95332](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95332)** `[ci] Move some test suites back to x86-64 runners`** — reverted by #95336, net no-op. When the codemod bumps the installed `eslint-config-next` to the target version, it now also bumps `eslint` to a version that satisfies the new `eslint-config-next`'s peer dependency (reads the package's `peerDependencies.eslint` range and resolves the highest matching `eslint@latest` from the registry). Without this, default CNA-15 projects hit `ERESOLVE` from `eslint-config-next`'s new peer dep and the codemod loop fails. Tested against the `cna-15.0.0-app` fixture; same agent-loop motivation as #95312. Full details in setup.md → `@next/codemod upgrade` also bumps eslint. **(R) [PR #95309](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95309) — `Rename Copy as prompt to Copy prompt, convert FixOption to FixCard`** by Aurora Scharff, merged 2026-07-01T14:34:46Z — public dev-overlay UI rename. The five non-material commits in canary.73 (test infrastructure, gitignore, CI workflow) are skipped. **Plus [React canary `19.3.0-canary-ec0fca31-20260701`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/react)** (cut 2026-07-01T16:30:24Z, replaces canary `e2731312-20260630`) — 5 commits ahead of the previous canary, one of which is material: **[React PR #36690](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/36690) `Add parentEnter/parentExit props to ViewTransition`** by Jack Pope, merged 2026-07-01T16:16:48Z — new experimental feature for orchestrating parent-level view transitions alongside child element transitions. The new `parentEnter` / `parentExit` props (on `<ViewTransition>`) take `className` / `name` arrays and apply a view transition at the *parent container* level when any of its children transition. Gated behind the `enableViewTransitionParentEnterExit` feature flag in React; set in `react/src/ReactFeatureFlags.js` (default off). Use case: animate the page chrome (header, side nav) when a child component cross-fades, so the chrome transitions with the child rather than staying still underneath. Full details in patterns.md → ViewTransition parentEnter/parentExit. The 17 commits since canary.70 split into **eight material user-facing changes** (six Next.js canary-branch PRs + one Turbopack PR + one React canary PR that became the new canary-`92f4fda3-20260629`): **(1) fix: preserve repeated search params in client page segment cache keys** ([#94863](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pulls/94863) by icyJoseph, merged June 29, 2026 at 11:54:29Z — silent stale-UI bug where `Object.fromEntries(new URLSearchParams(search))` collapsed `?foo=bar&foo=baz` to `{foo: ‘baz’}` in the client page segment cache key, causing cache collisions between `?color=red&color=blue` and `?color=blue`; affected `<Link>`, `router.replace`, `<form>` (next/form) soft-nav with repeated keys; fixed in `ppr-navigations.ts` + `segment-cache/scheduler.ts` + `route-params.ts` by switching to the existing `urlSearchParamsToParsedUrlQuery` helper which preserves repeated params as arrays; fixes issues [#92787](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/issues/92787) (filed against next 16.2.1-canary.38, April 14), [#93104](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/issues/93104) (filed against stable, April 21), and [#94821](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/issues/94821) (filed against canary.51, June 15 — next/form submission case); new e2e test at `test/e2e/app-dir/multi-value-search-params-stale/`; no API change, no config change, no opt-in flag; single-value URLs unaffected; **upgrade to canary.71+ or 16.3.0 stable to get the fix**; full details in routing.md); **(2) fix: client nav URL bar stuck on original URL after middleware redirect + rewrite-to-dynamic-route** ([#95207](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pulls/95207) by acdlite / Andrew Clark, merged June 29, 2026 at 17:00:28Z — silent stale-URL bug where a click on `<Link href="/a">` with a proxy/middleware redirect `/a → /` AND rewrite `/ → /a` (where `/a` is a dynamic route reading `await params`) rendered the correct page content but left `window.location.href` stuck on `/a` instead of `/`; only fires on soft-nav, hard-nav was always correct via the server resolver; root cause was the optimistic-URL-commit in the Instant Navigations reducer — the prediction was committed up front and the resolved RSC payload (after the redirect) was treated as confirmation of the prediction when the redirect target had the same shape as the prediction; fix invalidates the prediction on a redirect discovered during navigation and re-resolves the route before the address bar is shown; touches `ppr-navigations.ts` + `reducers/server-patch-reducer.ts` + `router-reducer-types.ts`; new e2e test at `test/e2e/app-dir/redirect-rewrite-dynamic/`; **fixes [issue #95195](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/issues/95195) by amannn against 16.3.0-preview.5** with a [public Playwright reproduction repo](https://github.com/amannn/nextjs-16-3-bug-repro-redirect); full details in routing.md); **(3) Turbopack `experimental.cssChunking: ‘graph’` config schema breaking change** ([#95088](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95088) by sokra / Tobias Koppers, merged June 29, 2026 at 15:53:24Z — `moduleFactorCost` option REMOVED, replaced by `weightDistribution`; new per-group cost formula `chunk_group_weight * (chunk_size + request_cost)` where `chunk_group_weight = group_total_size ^ (-weightDistribution)`; defaults retuned: `requestCost` `20000` → `100000` bytes, `weightDistribution` default `0.1`; old `{ type: ‘graph’, requestCost, moduleFactorCost }` config throws a schema error in canary.71+ — string form `‘graph’` is unchanged and uses the new defaults automatically; full details in performance.md). **Plus [React canary `19.3.0-canary-92f4fda3-20260629`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/react) (cut 2026-06-29T17:11:25Z, replaces canary `68631c04-20260626`)** — 3 commits ahead of the previous canary, one of which is material. *(As of 2026-06-30T16:54:01Z, the React canary SHA has moved forward to `19.3.0-canary-e2731312-20260630` (replaces `92f4fda3-20260629`). The new canary is non-material for the skill — its 6 commits are devtools-facade work and a chrome-devtools-mcp integration; no new APIs or fixes relevant to documented patterns.)* **[React PR #36851](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/36851) `[Float] Forward nonce option in ReactDOM.preloadModule()`** by udit / Udit Dewan, merged June 29, 2026 at 11:33:22Z — silent CSP violation where `ReactDOM.preloadModule(href, { nonce, as, ... })` accepted `nonce` in its public type but the implementation only forwarded `as`, `crossOrigin`, and `integrity` to the host dispatcher, dropping `nonce`; under strict `script-src ‘nonce-...’` CSP, the emitted `<link rel="modulepreload">` had no nonce and was blocked by the browser; `ReactDOM.preload()` and `ReactDOM.preinitModule()` already forwarded `nonce` correctly; 5 files, +37/-5 across `ReactDOMFloat.js`, `ReactDOMTypes.js`, `ReactFlightClientConfigDOM.js`, and the test suite; full details in security.md). The other 6 canary-branch commits since canary.70 are non-material (skill docs, CI, React upgrade bump, type improvement, internal Turbopack): `cc-adoption` skill tighten by Aurora Scharff ([#95225](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95225), docs-only), `unstable_rethrow` client-bundling fix by eps1lon ([#95196](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95196), internal bundling optimization, Closes #95189), Turbopack `server-reference-manifest` dedup by Tariqul ([#95242](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95242), internal bug fix), CC test command display fix by eps1lon ([#95267](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95267), CI), React canary bump in Next.js by Vercel Release Bot ([#95211](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95211), package.json only), `decodeReply` typing by mischnic ([#95263](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95263), type narrowing), `[changelog] Add 19.0.x, 19.1.x, 19.2.x patch releases` by eps1lon ([#36898](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/36898), changelog only), `[test] coverage for multi-byte code units in stream APIs` by eps1lon ([#36900](https://github.com/facebook/react/pull/36900), test only); **16.3.0-canary.70** released June 28, 2026 (npm dist-tag pointer moved 2026-06-29T00:02:51Z) — only one new content commit ([#95232](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95232) by mischnic, `Turbopack: make hash_xxh3_hash128 return u128` — pure Rust internal type-narrowing refactor, zero user-facing impact, all files under `turbopack/crates/`); **16.3.0-canary.69** released June 26, 2026 ([`4b164d7`](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/releases/tag/v16.3.0-canary.69), 15 commits since canary.68) — **fix: preserve middleware rewrite query in Pages API routes** ([#94905](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pulls/94905), fixes issue [#94647](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/issues/94647), June 10 — middleware rewrites from `/foo/bar?key=value` to `/api/proxy/bar?added=1&extra=2` were silently dropping the rewrite-added `added`/`extra` query params AND the destination catch-all `slug` param from `req.query` in Pages API handlers; regression from Next.js 14.2.35 introduced during the Pages API handler-interface migration, affected both webpack and Turbopack; `NextNodeServer.runApi` now forwards resolved `query` and `match.params` through the bundled handler context while preserving the original `req.url`; full details in routing.md), **hard-navigate to app routes shadowed by a pages dynamic route** ([#95185](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pulls/95185), shipped in canary.69 — soft-nav misroute fix, full details in routing.md), dev overlay Copy-as-prompt body restructured into step-by-step checklist ([#95186](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95186)), docs: insights error pages restructure + link to preview docs ([#95193](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95193)), docs: reorder blocking-prerender-dynamic fixes to Stream/Cache/Block ([#95198](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95198)), `next-dev-loop` requires `agent-browser >= 0.31.1` ([#95209](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95209)), Turbopack Rust toolchain bump ([#95205](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95205)), docs: fix preview message typo ([#95050](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95050)), Turbopack internal: revert `final_read_hint` ([#95178](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/pull/95178)) — first canary cut after the preview.5 stabilisation, so #94905 + #95185 are the two user-facing changes; everything else is docs/dev-overlay/agent-skill/tooling/internal metrics)
- **React 19.2.8** stable (shipped 2026-07-21T15:49:09Z, [GitHub release](https://github.com/facebook/react/releases/tag/v19.2.8); 19.2.7 → 19.2.8 diff is PR #37087 `[FlightReply] Performance improvements when decoding` + PR #36753 housekeeping) + **React 19.2.x canary** [`81e442ea-20260721`](https://www.npmjs.com/package/react?activeTab=canary) (ships Fragment-blur fix + nested ViewTransition) — React Compiler 1.0 stable, `use()` hook, `useOptimistic`, `useFormStatus`, `useActionState`, `useEffectEvent`, `cacheSignal`, `cache`, `<Activity>`
- **TypeScript 6.0.3** (strict by default, ES2026 target, import defer) + **TypeScript 7.0.1-rc** (June 18, 2026, RC release of Go-based compiler ships as the main `typescript` package — `npx tsc` is now Go, ~10× faster, Strada internal API; stable targeted within 2 months of RC)
- **Zod 4.4.3** (14x faster string parsing, strict/loose object modes, `z.file()`, `z.templateLiteral()`)
- **Tailwind CSS v4.3.2** + **shadcn/ui 4.13.0** (CSS-first config via `@theme` directive — no tailwind.config.js by default; **shadcn 4.13.0 (July 3, 2026)** flips the default headless lib to **Base UI** (Radix still supported as the explicit alternative; per [official changelog](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/changelog/2026-07-base-ui-default) this is the result of wide community adoption); **shadcn/typeset (July 14, 2026)** — new stream-friendly typography system (single CSS file, three CSS variables per container, no package, no config layer, container-aware, pairs naturally with shadcn 4.12.0 Chat Components for streaming markdown in chat UIs); **v4.3.2 (June 29, 2026) pure bug-fix patch** — `auto-rows-*` / `auto-cols-*` accept bare numeric values (#20229), `@tailwindcss/cli --watch` no longer crashes on Windows when `@source` points to a missing directory (#20242), `@tailwindcss/vite` no longer crashes in Deno v2.8.x (#20245), `@tailwindcss/cli --watch` no longer re-emits an unchanged file when only a sibling changes (#20249); v4.3.1 patch added `--silent` CLI flag, `@apply` with CSS mixins, cleaner spacing output; **4.11.1 (June 26, 2026) patches `shadcn add` to preserve existing `package.json` dependency specifiers** — fixes [shadcn-ui/ui#10525](https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/issues/10525) where the CLI could swap specifier values between packages, leaving lockfile + manifest contradictory; also drops the `node-fetch` transitive dep in favor of native `fetch` ([#10905](https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/pull/10905)); **4.12.0 (June 26, 2026, same day as 4.11.1) ships the first phase of Chat Components** — `MessageScroller` / `Message` / `Bubble` / `Attachment` / `Marker` (each available in Base UI + Radix UI flavors), two new CSS utilities `scroll-fade` + `shimmer` (shipped via `shadcn/tailwind.css`, so any project initialized with `npx shadcn@latest init` gets them for free), and a brand-new npm package **`@shadcn/react@0.1.0`** — unstyled headless React primitives, first primitive is `@shadcn/react/message-scroller` with the same interaction logic the registry item wraps)
- **Modern CSS 2026** — `field-sizing: content` (Baseline 2026, Firefox 152 just shipped), `@starting-style` (Baseline 2024), CSS Anchor Positioning (cross-engine 2025–26), OKLCH color space, `color-scheme` + `light-dark()` function, scroll-driven animations
